[409] h2: Appendix K Proof of Theorem 5

[410] p: We construct both a target distribution q 0 q_{0} and a special form of the estimated predictive model μ θ \mu_{\theta} , which yield a lower error bound.

[411] p: To proceed, fix 𝒂 , 𝒃 ∈ [ S ] d \bm{a},\bm{b}\in[S]^{d} where 𝒃 i ≠ 𝒂 i \bm{b}^{i}\neq\bm{a}^{i} and 𝒂 i , 𝒃 i ≠ [ MASK ] \bm{a}^{i},\bm{b}^{i}\neq\mathrm{[MASK]} for all i ∈ [ d ] i\in[d] . Let q 0 = 𝜹 𝒂 q_{0}=\bm{\delta}_{\bm{a}} . Also, for any x t x_{t} , t t , and i i , let μ θ \mu_{\theta} be as follows:

[412] table: μ θ i ​ ( x t , t ) ​ [ y i ] = { 1 − ρ , if ​ y i = 𝒂 i , ρ , if ​ y i = 𝒃 i . \mu_{\theta}^{i}(x_{t},t)[y^{i}]=\begin{cases}1-\rho,&\text{ if }y^{i}=\bm{a}^{i},\\ \rho,&\text{ if }y^{i}=\bm{b}^{i}.\end{cases}

[413] p: where we choose choose ρ = 1 − e − ε ′′ score / d \rho=1-e^{-\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{score}}/d} .

[414] p: We next show that the above constructed μ θ \mu_{\theta} satisfies Assumption 2 , i.e., ∫ 0 + ∞ ℒ S ​ E ​ ( s , t ) ​ 𝑑 t ≤ ε score ′′ \int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathcal{L}_{SE}(s;t)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{score}} . First note that under q 0 = 𝜹 𝒂 q_{0}=\bm{\delta}_{\bm{a}} , we have q 0 l ( ⋅ | x 0 ℳ k c ) = 𝜹 𝒂 l l q_{0}^{l}(\cdot|x_{0}^{\mathcal{M}_{k}^{c}})=\bm{\delta}^{l}_{\bm{a}^{l}} . Then, we can apply Proposition 2 and compute the integrated ℒ S ​ E \mathcal{L}_{SE} as follows.

[415] table: ∫ 0 + ∞ ℒ S ​ E ​ ( s , t ) ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathcal{L}_{SE}(s;t)\,\mathrm{d}t = 𝔼 x 0 ∼ q 0 [ ℒ ∞ ( x 0 ) ] − ∑ k = 1 d 1 k 𝔼 x 0 ∼ q 0 𝔼 ℳ k ∑ l ∈ ℳ k H ( q 0 l ( ⋅ | x 0 ℳ k c ) ) \displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{x_{0}\sim q_{0}}[\mathcal{L}_{\infty}(x_{0})]-\sum_{k=1}^{d}\frac{1}{k}\mathbb{E}_{x_{0}\sim q_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{M}_{k}}\sum_{l\in\mathcal{M}_{k}}H\left(q_{0}^{l}(\cdot|x_{0}^{\mathcal{M}_{k}^{c}})\right) = ℒ ∞ ​ ( 𝒂 ) − 0 \displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\infty}(\bm{a})-0 = ∫ 0 + ∞ − α t 1 − α t 𝔼 q t | 0 ​ ( x t | 𝒂 ) [ ∑ l : x t l = [ MASK ] log ( μ θ l ( x t , t ) [ 𝒂 l ] ) ] d t \displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{-\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}\mathbb{E}_{q_{t|0}(x_{t}|\bm{a})}\left[\sum_{l:x_{t}^{l}=\mathrm{[MASK]}}\log\left(\mu_{\theta}^{l}(x_{t},t)[\bm{a}^{l}]\right)\right]\mathrm{d}t = ∫ 0 + ∞ − α t 1 − α t 𝔼 q t | 0 ​ ( x t | 𝒂 ) [ ∑ l : x t l = [ MASK ] log ( 1 − ρ ) ] d t \displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{-\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}\mathbb{E}_{q_{t|0}(x_{t}|\bm{a})}\left[\sum_{l:x_{t}^{l}=\mathrm{[MASK]}}\log\left(1-\rho\right)\right]\mathrm{d}t = ∫ 0 + ∞ − α t 1 − α t ​ log ⁡ ( 1 − ρ ) ​ 𝔼 q t | 0 ​ ( x t | 𝒂 ) ​ [ m ⁡ ( x t ) ] ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{-\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}\log\left(1-\rho\right)\mathbb{E}_{q_{t|0}(x_{t}|\bm{a})}\left[m(x_{t})\right]\mathrm{d}t = ∫ 0 + ∞ − α t 1 − α t ​ log ⁡ ( 1 − ρ ) ​ d ​ ( 1 − α t ) ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{-\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}\log\left(1-\rho\right)d(1-\alpha_{t})\mathrm{d}t = − d ​ log ⁡ ( 1 − ρ ) = ε score ′′ \displaystyle=-d\log(1-\rho)=\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{score}}

[416] p: where m ⁡ ( x t ) m(x_{t}) denotes the number of [ MASK ] \mathrm{[MASK]} tokens in x t x_{t} , and the last inequality follows from the choice of ρ \rho .

[417] p: Now, under FHS, since each token is predicted exactly once and μ θ \mu_{\theta} predicts the correct token 𝒂 i \bm{a}^{i} at each position i i with the probability ( 1 − ρ ) (1-\rho) , we have ℙ FHS ​ ( 𝒂 ) = ( 1 − ρ ) d \mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{FHS}}(\bm{a})=(1-\rho)^{d} . Then, since q 0 = 𝜹 𝒂 q_{0}=\bm{\delta}_{\bm{a}} , it follows that

[418] table: KL ( q 0 | | ℙ FHS ) = − log ( ℙ FHS ( a ) ) = − d log ( 1 − ρ ) = ε score ′′ \mathrm{KL}(q_{0}||\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{FHS}})=-\log\left(\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{FHS}}(a)\right)=-d\log(1-\rho)=\varepsilon^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{score}} (39)

[419] p: where the last inequality follows from the choice of ρ \rho .

[420] p: The proof is now complete. Note that this special case with such a q 0 q_{0} and μ θ \mu_{\theta} indicates that the upper bound argued in Theorem 4 is tight.

[421] h2: Appendix L Proofs of Auxiliary Lemmas

[422] p: In this section, we provide all the proofs for the auxiliary lemmas used in this paper.

[423] h3: L.1 Proof of Lemma 1

[424] p: Recall that ( T − t k ) − ( T − t k + 1 ) = κ ​ min ⁡ { 1 , T − t k } (T-t_{k})-(T-t_{k+1})=\kappa\min\left\{1,T-t_{k}\right\} . Define k ∗ := sup { k : T − t k > 1 } k^{*}:=\sup\left\{k:T-t_{k}>1\right\} . We thus have the following cases.

[425] p: Case 1: k < k ∗ k<k^{*} . This implies that T − t k > T − t k + 1 > 1 T-t_{k}>T-t_{k+1}>1 , and t k + 1 − t k = κ t_{k+1}-t_{k}=\kappa . Thus, we have

[426] table: ∑ k = 0 k ∗ − 1 ( t k + 1 − t k ) p min ⁡ { 1 , T − t k + 1 } p = κ p ​ k ∗ . \sum_{k=0}^{k^{*}-1}\frac{(t_{k+1}-t_{k})^{p}}{\min\left\{1,T-t_{k+1}\right\}^{p}}=\kappa^{p}k^{*}.

[427] p: Case 2: k > k ∗ k>k^{*} . This implies that T − t k + 1 < T − t k ≤ 1 T-t_{k+1}<T-t_{k}\leq 1 , and

[428] table: ∑ k = k ∗ + 1 N − 1 ( t k + 1 − t k ) p min ⁡ { 1 , T − t k + 1 } p \displaystyle\sum_{k=k^{*}+1}^{N-1}\frac{(t_{k+1}-t_{k})^{p}}{\min\left\{1,T-t_{k+1}\right\}^{p}} = κ p ​ ∑ k = k ∗ + 1 N − 1 ( T − t k T − t k + 1 ) p \displaystyle=\kappa^{p}\sum_{k=k^{*}+1}^{N-1}\left(\frac{T-t_{k}}{T-t_{k+1}}\right)^{p} = κ p ​ ∑ k = k ∗ + 1 N − 1 ( 1 1 − κ ) p \displaystyle=\kappa^{p}\sum_{k=k^{*}+1}^{N-1}\left(\frac{1}{1-\kappa}\right)^{p} ≍ κ p ​ ( N − k ∗ − 1 ) \displaystyle\asymp\kappa^{p}(N-k^{*}-1)

[429] p: where the first line follows by definition of t k + 1 t_{k+1} , and the last line follows due to the following. Note that for Case 2, the step-sizes satisfies that

[430] table: T − t k ≤ T − t k + 1 + κ . T-t_{k}\leq T-t_{k+1}+\kappa.

[431] p: Since T − t k + 1 ≥ δ − 1 T-t_{k+1}\geq\delta^{-1} , this implies that 1 ≤ T − t k T − t k + 1 = 1 + O ⁡ ( κ ) = 1 + o ⁡ ( 1 ) 1\leq\frac{T-t_{k}}{T-t_{k+1}}=1+O(\kappa)=1+o(1) .

[432] p: Case 3: k = k ∗ k=k^{*} . This implies that T − t k + 1 ∈ ( 1 − κ , 1 ] T-t_{k+1}\in(1-\kappa,1] , T − t k ∈ ( 1 , 1 + κ ] T-t_{k}\in(1,1+\kappa] , and t k + 1 − t k ≤ κ t_{k+1}-t_{k}\leq\kappa . For this case, we can similarly get T − t k T − t k + 1 = 1 + o ⁡ ( 1 ) \frac{T-t_{k}}{T-t_{k+1}}=1+o(1) , and thus

[433] table: ( t k + 1 − t k ) p min ⁡ { 1 , T − t k + 1 } p = κ p ​ ( 1 + o ⁡ ( 1 ) ) . \frac{(t_{k+1}-t_{k})^{p}}{\min\left\{1,T-t_{k+1}\right\}^{p}}=\kappa^{p}(1+o(1)).

[434] p: Summing up all three cases, we have

[435] table: ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ( t k + 1 − t k ) p min ⁡ { 1 , T − t k + 1 } p ≍ κ p ​ N . \sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\frac{(t_{k+1}-t_{k})^{p}}{\min\left\{1,T-t_{k+1}\right\}^{p}}\asymp\kappa^{p}N.

[436] p: Next, we need to determine the total number of steps. When k ≤ k ∗ k\leq k^{*} , the number of steps is simply

[437] table: k ∗ = : N 1 = ⌊ T − 1 κ ⌋ ≍ T κ . k^{*}=:N_{1}=\left\lfloor\frac{T-1}{\kappa}\right\rfloor\asymp\frac{T}{\kappa}.

[438] p: When k > k ∗ k>k^{*} , in order to reach that T − t N ≍ δ T-t_{N}\asymp\delta , we need

[439] table: N 2 ≍ log 1 − κ ⁡ δ ≍ log ⁡ δ − 1 κ . N_{2}\asymp\log_{1-\kappa}\delta\asymp\frac{\log\delta^{-1}}{\kappa}.

[440] p: Therefore, the total number of steps satisfies that

[441] table: N = N 1 + N 2 ≍ T + log ⁡ δ − 1 κ . N=N_{1}+N_{2}\asymp\frac{T+\log\delta^{-1}}{\kappa}.

[442] p: The proof is now complete.

[443] h3: L.2 Proof of Lemma 2

[444] p: Before we dive into the proof, we provide some intuition for our result. Intuitively, the instantaneous change of the indicator function should only be non-zero for those x x on the boundary (i.e., where q → t ​ ( x ) = p t ​ ( x ) \mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}(x)=p_{t}(x) ). As a result, this second term should be exactly zero. This is characterized by the following lemma.

[445] p: Write Φ t := { x : q → t ​ ( x ) ≠ p t ​ ( x ) } \Phi_{t}:=\{x:\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}(x)\neq p_{t}(x)\} . Suppose that we can show that for all such x ∈ Φ t x\in\Phi_{t} we have ∂ ∂ t 𝟙 { q → t ( x ) > p t ( x ) } = 0 \frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathds{1}\left\{\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}(x)>p_{t}(x)\right\}=0 , then the result is obvious.

[446] p: Now fix x ∈ Φ t x\in\Phi_{t} . From the Kolmogorov forward equation, we have

[447] table: q → t + Δ ​ t ​ ( x ) \displaystyle\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t+\Delta t}(x) = ∑ y ∈ 𝒳 q → t ( y ) ( 𝟙 { y = x } + R → t ( y , x ) Δ t ) + o ( Δ t ) , \displaystyle=\sum_{y\in\mathcal{X}}\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}(y)\left(\mathds{1}\left\{y=x\right\}+\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle R\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle R\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle R\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle R\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}(y,x)\Delta t\right)+o(\Delta t), p t + Δ ​ t ​ ( x ) \displaystyle p_{t+\Delta t}(x) = ∑ y ∈ 𝒳 p t ( y ) ( 𝟙 { y = x } + R ^ t ( y , x ) Δ t ) + o ( Δ t ) . \displaystyle=\sum_{y\in\mathcal{X}}p_{t}(y)\left(\mathds{1}\left\{y=x\right\}+\hat{R}_{t}(y,x)\Delta t\right)+o(\Delta t).

[448] p: Thus,

[449] table: q → t + Δ ​ t ​ ( x ) − p t + Δ ​ t ​ ( x ) = q → t ​ ( x ) − p t ​ ( x ) + O ⁡ ( Δ ​ t ) . \mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t+\Delta t}(x)-p_{t+\Delta t}(x)=\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}(x)-p_{t}(x)+O(\Delta t).

[450] p: We now divide into the following two cases within Φ t \Phi_{t} . The first case is where q → t ​ ( x ) > p t ​ ( x ) \mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}(x)>p_{t}(x) . Since 𝒳 \mathcal{X} is discrete, there exists some ϵ t > 0 \epsilon_{t}>0 such that q → t ​ ( x ) − p t ​ ( x ) ≥ ϵ t \mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}(x)-p_{t}(x)\geq\epsilon_{t} . Then, for vanishing Δ ​ t \Delta t , q → t + Δ ​ t ​ ( x ) − p t + Δ ​ t ​ ( x ) ≥ ϵ t − O ⁡ ( Δ ​ t ) > 0 \mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t+\Delta t}(x)-p_{t+\Delta t}(x)\geq\epsilon_{t}-O(\Delta t)>0 . The second case is where q → t ​ ( x ) < p t ​ ( x ) \mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}(x)<p_{t}(x) . With a similar argument, we get q → t + Δ ​ t ​ ( x ) < p t + Δ ​ t ​ ( x ) \mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle q\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle q\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t+\Delta t}(x)<p_{t+\Delta t}(x) . For both cases, we get, for vanishing Δ ​ t \Delta t and x ∈ Φ t x\in\Phi_{t} ,

[452] p: The proof is now complete.

[453] h3: L.3 Proof of Lemma 3

[454] p: Note that p 0 = 𝜹 [ MASK ] d p_{0}=\bm{\delta}_{\mathrm{[MASK]}^{d}} if we use the absorbing rate matrix. Also, a useful result under the absorbing-rate is that we can explicitly write the forward conditional probability as

[455] table: q t | 0 ​ ( x | x 0 ) = ( 1 − e − t ) ∑ i 𝟙 ​ { x i = [ MASK ] , x 0 i ≠ [ MASK ] } ⋅ ( e − t ) ∑ i 𝟙 { x i = x 0 i ≠ [ MASK ] } ⋅ 0 ∑ i 𝟙 ​ { x i ≠ [ MASK ] , x i ≠ x 0 i } . q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})=(1-e^{-t})^{\sum_{i}\mathds{1}\left\{x^{i}=\mathrm{[MASK]},x_{0}^{i}\neq\mathrm{[MASK]}\right\}}\\ \cdot(e^{-t})^{\sum_{i}\mathds{1}\left\{x^{i}=x_{0}^{i}\neq\mathrm{[MASK]}\right\}}\cdot 0^{\sum_{i}\mathds{1}\left\{x^{i}\neq\mathrm{[MASK]},x^{i}\neq x_{0}^{i}\right\}}. (40)

Now, for uniform-rate, we have R t ( x , x ) = − ∑ y : y ≠ x R t ( x , y ) = − S − 1 S d R_{t}(x,x)=-\sum_{y:y\neq x}R_{t}(x,y)=-\frac{S-1}{S}d . Also, for absorbing-rate, we have R t ( x , x ) = − ∑ y : y ≠ x R t ( x , y ) = − ( d − m ( x ) ) R_{t}(x,x)=-\sum_{y:y\neq x}R_{t}(x,y)=-(d-m(x)) , where m ⁡ ( x ) ( ≤ d ) m(x)\penalty\ (\leq d) is the number of mask states in x x . We have the desired result for both cases.

[463] h3: L.5 Proof of Lemma 5

[464] p: (1) Instantaneous total rate of leaving x x at time t t . For any t t and x ≠ y x\neq y , the reverse CTMC process satisfies the local transition given by

[465] table: ℙ ⁡ ( Y t − d ​ t = y ∣ Y t = x ) = R ~ t ​ ( x , y ) ​ d ​ t + o ⁡ ( d ​ t ) . \mathbb{P}(Y_{t-dt}=y\mid Y_{t}=x)=\widetilde{R}_{t}(x,y)\,dt+o(dt). (41)

[466] p: Taking summation over all y ≠ x y\neq x , we obtain

[467] table: ℙ ⁡ ( Y t − d ​ t ≠ x ∣ Y t = x ) = ∑ y ≠ x R ~ t ​ ( x , y ) ​ d ​ t + o ⁡ ( d ​ t ) = Λ ⁡ ( t , x ) ​ d ​ t + o ⁡ ( d ​ t ) , \mathbb{P}(Y_{t-dt}\neq x\mid Y_{t}=x)=\sum_{y\neq x}\widetilde{R}_{t}(x,y)\,dt+o(dt)=\Lambda(t,x)\,dt+o(dt), (42)

[468] p: where

[469] table: Λ ⁡ ( t , x ) = ∑ y ≠ x R ~ t ​ ( x , y ) . \Lambda(t,x)=\sum_{y\neq x}\widetilde{R}_{t}(x,y).

[470] p: Thus Λ ⁡ ( t , x ) \Lambda(t,x) is the instantaneous rate of leaving state x x at time t t .

[471] p: (2) Unmasking time τ \tau in state x x . Fix t t and x x , and define the survival function

[472] table: S ⁡ ( h ) := ℙ ⁡ ( τ < t − h ∣ Y t = x ) , h ≥ 0 , S(h):=\mathbb{P}(\tau<t-h\mid Y_{t}=x),\qquad h\geq 0,

[473] p: where

[474] table: τ := sup { s < t : Y s ≠ x } \tau:=\sup\{s<t:Y_{s}\neq x\}

[475] p: is the first unmasking time after t t for the reverse process indexed by the forward time.

[476] p: To derive a differential equation for S ⁡ ( h ) S(h) , let δ > 0 \delta>0 be a small constant, and 𝟏 { ⋅ } \mathbf{1}_{\{\cdot\}} denote the indicator function. Using the Markov property at time t − h t-h , we have

[477] table: S ⁡ ( h + δ ) \displaystyle S(h+\delta) = ℙ ⁡ ( τ < t − h − δ | Y t = x ) \displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\tau<t-h-\delta|Y_{t}=x) = 𝔼 τ [ 𝟏 { τ < t − h } 𝟏 { τ < t − h − δ } | Y t = x ] \displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\tau}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau<t-h\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau<t-h-\delta\}}|Y_{t}=x\right] = 𝔼 Y t − h [ 𝔼 τ | Y t − h [ 𝟏 { τ < t − h } 𝟏 { τ < t − h − δ } | Y t − h , Y t = x ] | Y t = x ] \displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Y_{t-h}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\tau|Y_{t-h}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau<t-h\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau<t-h-\delta\}}|Y_{t-h},Y_{t}=x\right]|Y_{t}=x\right] = ( i ) 𝔼 Y t − h [ 𝔼 τ | Y t − h [ 𝟏 { τ < t − h } 𝟏 { τ < t − h − δ } | Y t − h = x , Y t = x ] | Y t = x ] \displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\mathbb{E}_{Y_{t-h}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\tau|Y_{t-h}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau<t-h\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau<t-h-\delta\}}|Y_{t-h}=x,Y_{t}=x\right]|Y_{t}=x\right] = ( i ​ i ) 𝔼 Y t − h [ 𝔼 τ | Y t − h [ 𝟏 { τ < t − h } | Y t − h = x , Y t = x ] ℙ ( τ < t − h − δ | Y t − h = x ) | Y t = x ] \displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\mathbb{E}_{Y_{t-h}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\tau|Y_{t-h}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau<t-h\}}|Y_{t-h}=x,Y_{t}=x\right]\mathbb{P}(\tau<t-h-\delta|Y_{t-h}=x)|Y_{t}=x\right] = 𝔼 Y t − h [ 𝔼 τ | Y t − h [ 𝟏 { τ < t − h } | Y t − h = x , Y t = x ] | Y t = x ] ℙ ( τ < t − h − δ | Y t − h = x ) \displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Y_{t-h}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\tau|Y_{t-h}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau<t-h\}}|Y_{t-h}=x,Y_{t}=x\right]|Y_{t}=x\right]\mathbb{P}(\tau<t-h-\delta|Y_{t-h}=x) = ( i ​ i ​ i ) 𝔼 Y t − h [ 𝔼 τ | Y t − h [ 𝟏 { τ < t − h } | Y t − h , Y t = x ] | Y t = x ] ℙ ( τ < t − h − δ | Y t − h = x ) \displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{=}\mathbb{E}_{Y_{t-h}}\left[\mathbb{E}_{\tau|Y_{t-h}}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau<t-h\}}|Y_{t-h},Y_{t}=x\right]|Y_{t}=x\right]\mathbb{P}(\tau<t-h-\delta|Y_{t-h}=x) = 𝔼 τ [ 𝟏 { τ < t − h } | Y t = x ] ℙ ( τ < t − h − δ | Y t − h = x ) , \displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\tau}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\{\tau<t-h\}}|Y_{t}=x\right]\mathbb{P}(\tau<t-h-\delta|Y_{t-h}=x),

[478] p: Here ( i ) (i) follows because 𝟏 { τ < t − h } \mathbf{1}_{\{\tau<t-h\}} is non-zero only when Y t − h = Y t = x Y_{t-h}=Y_{t}=x . Also, ( i ​ i ) (ii) follows because 𝟏 { τ < t − h } \mathbf{1}_{\{\tau<t-h\}} is a (deterministic) function of Y t − h Y_{t-h} and Y t Y_{t} . Also, by Markov property, ℙ ⁡ ( τ < t − h − δ | Y t − h , Y t ) = ℙ ⁡ ( τ < t − h − δ | Y t − h ) \mathbb{P}(\tau<t-h-\delta|Y_{t-h},Y_{t})=\mathbb{P}(\tau<t-h-\delta|Y_{t-h}) . Then, ( i ​ i ​ i ) (iii) follows again because 𝟏 { τ < t − h } \mathbf{1}_{\{\tau<t-h\}} is non-zero only when Y t − h = Y t = x Y_{t-h}=Y_{t}=x . Thus,

[479] table: S ⁡ ( h + δ ) \displaystyle S(h+\delta) = S ⁡ ( h ) ​ ℙ ​ ( τ < t − h − δ ∣ Y t − h = x ) \displaystyle=S(h)\,\mathbb{P}(\tau<t-h-\delta\mid Y_{t-h}=x) = S ⁡ ( h ) ​ ( 1 − Λ ⁡ ( t − h , x ) ​ δ + o ⁡ ( δ ) ) \displaystyle=S(h)\big(1-\Lambda(t-h,x)\,\delta+o(\delta)\big) (43)

[480] p: where the second equality follows because given Y t − h = x Y_{t-h}=x , the condition τ < t − h − δ \tau<t-h-\delta exactly defines the process that does not leave x x in [ t − h − δ , t − h ] [t-h-\delta,\,t-h] , and hence ( 42 ) yields

[481] table: ℙ ⁡ ( τ < t − h − δ ∣ Y t − h = x ) = 1 − Λ ⁡ ( t − h , x ) ​ δ + o ⁡ ( δ ) . \mathbb{P}(\tau<t-h-\delta\mid Y_{t-h}=x)=1-\Lambda(t-h,x)\,\delta+o(\delta).

[482] p: Equation ( 43 ) further yields

[483] table: S ⁡ ( h + δ ) − S ⁡ ( h ) δ = − Λ ⁡ ( t − h , x ) ​ S ​ ( h ) + o ⁡ ( 1 ) , \frac{S(h+\delta)-S(h)}{\delta}=-\Lambda(t-h,x)\,S(h)+o(1),

[484] p: which implies the following ODE as δ → 0 \delta\to 0 :

[485] table: S ′ ​ ( h ) = − Λ ⁡ ( t − h , x ) ​ S ​ ( h ) , S ⁡ ( 0 ) = 1 . S^{\prime}(h)=-\Lambda(t-h,x)\,S(h),\qquad S(0)=1.

[486] p: Solving the above ODE, we get

[487] table: S ( h ) = exp ( − ∫ h 0 Λ ( t − u , x ) d u ) = exp ( − ∫ t − h t Λ ( u , x ) d u ) , S(h)=\exp\!\Big(-\int_{h}^{0}\Lambda(t-u,x)\,du\Big)=\exp\!\Big(-\int_{t-h}^{t}\Lambda(u,x)\,du\Big),

[488] p: which completes the proof of the second claim.

[489] p: (3) Distribution of the predicted token. We first derive:

[490] table: ℙ \displaystyle\mathbb{P} ( Y τ = y ∣ Y t = x , τ ∈ ( t − δ , t ] ) = ( Y τ = y ∣ Y t = x ) ℙ ⁡ ( Y τ ≠ x ∣ Y t = x ) = R ~ t ​ ( x , y ) ​ δ + o ⁡ ( δ ) Λ ⁡ ( t , x ) ​ δ + o ⁡ ( δ ) . \displaystyle(Y_{\tau}=y\mid Y_{t}=x,\tau\in(t-\delta,t])=\frac{\mathbb{(}Y_{\tau}=y\mid Y_{t}=x)}{\mathbb{P}(Y_{\tau}\neq x\mid Y_{t}=x)}=\frac{\widetilde{R}_{t}(x,y)\delta+o(\delta)}{\Lambda(t,x)\delta+o(\delta)}.

[491] p: Then, letting δ ↓ 0 \delta\downarrow 0 , we have

[492] table: lim δ ↓ 0 ℙ ( Y τ = y ∣ Y t = x , τ ∈ ( t − δ , t ] ) = R ~ t ​ ( x , y ) Λ ⁡ ( t , x ) , \lim\limits_{\delta\downarrow 0}\ \mathbb{P}(Y_{\tau}=y\mid Y_{t}=x,\tau\in(t-\delta,t])=\frac{\widetilde{R}_{t}(x,y)}{\Lambda(t,x)},

[493] p: which implies the distribution of next state. The proof is now complete.

[494] h3: L.6 Proof of Lemma 6

[495] p: Following from the proof of Proposition 4.1 in [ 22 ] , we have

[496] table: 1 − α τ k − 1 = u ( k ) , 1-\alpha_{\tau_{k-1}}=u_{(k)},

[497] p: where { u ( k ) } k = 1 d \{u_{(k)}\}_{k=1}^{d} are order statistics of d d independent and uniformly distributed variables on [ 0 , 1 ] [0,1] , satisfying u ( d ) > ⋯ > u ( 1 ) u_{(d)}>\cdots>u_{(1)} . It is well known that the k k -th order statistic of d d i.i.d. random variables with the distribution of Unif ​ ( 0 , 1 ) \text{Unif}\;(0,1) follows a B ​ e ​ t ​ a ​ ( k , d + 1 − k ) Beta(k,d+1-k) distribution given by

[498] table: u ( k ) ∼ B ​ e ​ t ​ a ​ ( k , d + 1 − k ) . u_{(k)}\sim Beta(k,d+1-k).

[499] p: We use the following fact for the Beta distribution. Suppose that X ∼ B ​ e ​ t ​ a ​ ( a , b ) X\sim Beta(a,b) , then 1 − X ∼ B ​ e ​ t ​ a ​ ( b , a ) 1-X\sim Beta(b,a) . Thus, we obtain the distribution of α τ k − 1 \alpha_{\tau_{k-1}} as

[500] table: α τ k − 1 ∼ B ​ e ​ t ​ a ​ ( d + 1 − k , k ) . \alpha_{\tau_{k-1}}\sim Beta(d+1-k,k).

[501] p: The proof is now complete.

[502] h2: Instructions for reporting errors

[503] p: We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility and mobile support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the methods listed below:

[504] p: Tip: You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report.

[505] p: Our team has already identified the following issues . We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for all.

[506] p: Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a list of packages that need conversion , and welcome developer contributions .
